I’ll be trying to explain one thing, like the keys, and then I’m talking about jail, then a prayer rug, then chess pieces… and people tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying. It’s been bad enough that I figured I need help.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually high, excited, or full of energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes I do. I get this feeling like I’m real high up, like powerful, and I start laughing a lot and talking loud with everybody. But then if somebody questions me or says something wrong, I can snap quick and start shouting over something small.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices when your mood felt normal?\n\nPatient: Yes. There were calmer weeks where I wasn’t really high or real depressed, but I still heard him… the man in the vents. And I still thought people in the building were signaling about me. That went on more than two weeks, even when my mood felt kind of normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I’ve had stretches like that. Most days I’d just sit on the edge of the bed after morning prayer, feeling real heavy and empty. Like there wasn’t any point in cleaning myself up or even going outside.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the whole time you’ve been ill, have the voices or beliefs that people were signaling about you been present for most of that time?\n\nPatient: Most of the time, yeah, I’ve been in those real keyed-up stretches. I talk too much, I get these big cleaning plans in my head that I can’t really finish, and I feel driven, like I can’t slow myself down.\n\nDoctor: Over the whole time you've been ill, have those keyed-up or high-energy periods made up most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yes… most of the time it’s been like that. I get keyed up, talking too much, making these big cleaning plans I can’t really do, and I feel pushed, like I can’t slow myself down.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been hospitalized because you were so high, energized, or out of control?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t really have a clear answer for that. I’ve been out of control at times, but I don’t know if I was hospitalized for being high or energized like that.\n\nDoctor: When your mood feels normal, does your speech still get so mixed up that other people can’t follow you?\n\nPatient: Yeah. It sounded real, like he was right beside me.\n\nDoctor: Altogether, have the voices or confused speech been going on for at least 6 months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it’s been longer than six months. The voices and that feeling like I’m being watched or marked somehow… that’s been going on about a year and a half now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when feeling so high or energized caused serious problems in your life, like you couldn’t function normally?\n\nPatient: Yes. There have been times like that where I couldn’t keep up with normal life. I missed shifts, and I lost cleaning work because people said they couldn’t rely on me or understand what I was saying. I stopped going to mosque and family gatherings too. And a couple times it got unsafe enough that other people had to call for emergency help.\n\nDoctor: During those keyed-up times, did you need much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes. During those times I could sleep maybe two or three hours and still get up before dawn feeling wide awake. I’d start planning work and things I needed to do, like sleep was for other people, not me.\n\nDoctor: When your mood feels normal, do people say your facial expression or emotions seem flat or inappropriate?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. Nobody’s told me that in those words. When I feel normal, I think my face and emotions seem normal too, but I may not always notice how I look to other people.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had one of those very high-energy periods when you did not hear the man in the vents or feel that people were signaling about you?\n\nPatient: No, not really.
